#2853. Fantasy Football: From chasing history to breakouts, 1 thing to watch in all 32 WR rooms for the second half

With the second half of the season in full swing, here's what Yahoo analyst Matt Harmon is looking for in each team's WR room for fantasy football.

After a blistering start to the year, Flowers is now WR8 in yards per route run and WR16 in points per game. It was tracking to be so much better than this before quarterback chaos sunk the Ravens' passing game. In Jacoby Brissett's first start, the Ravens dropped back over 50 times, a rarity in the Lamar Jackson era, but the passing tree wasn't consolidated whatsoever. Flowers was targeted on 14.9% of his routes, the lowest rate of the season. Both fantasy managers and a Ravens team that desperately needs to win require the ball in Flowers' hands much more often than that, even if he ultimately fumbled the game away in Week 10.

When DJ Moore has been available for the Bills, all the mainstay passing-game players get a bump. On plays where Moore is on the field, Khalil Shakir averages 2.03 yards per route and Dalton Kincaid averages 3.15. Kincaid specifically drops to 1.97 when Moore isn't on the field. This is because Moore, who averages a whopping 16.6 air yards per target, has been a huge add as a coverage-dictator and vertical threat. Moore isn't going to be a WR1-level producer but he's key to the health of this offense and is capable of spike weeks in this ecosystem.

Right now, the Bengals have the WR4 and WR6 in points per game on their roster. Ja'Marr Chase and Tee Higgins have been masterful this season with Joe Burrow playing the best football of his career. Each receiver has five games inside the top-10 of weekly fantasy scoring. The Bengals offense has used condensed formations, and the leap in their offensive line's pass-blocking has been a key factor. Quietly, Dohnte Meyers is emerging as a credible option as the WR3 who can play multiple positions, which has also allowed them to be more creative with the deployment. I see no reason this train slows down any time soon.

So far this season, Denzel Boston has been used as a downfield X-receiver and KC Concepcion has been a movement option who runs over 60% of his routes from the slot. I'm not critiquing that deployment, as both are excellent options in those roles and have been fantastic to start their rookie seasons. However, I believe Boston is capable of being weaponized with some reps as a big slot and Concepcion can win outside against man coverage. If those roles start to blend down the stretch, or especially in future seasons, these guys can be even more productive.

Call it hope or call it cope, but we can blame my favorite strength-of-schedule zealot, Justin Boone, for even getting me to keep the light on. Coming out of the bye, the Broncos have games against the Raiders (twice), Steelers, Jets, Dolphins, Bills and Patriots. Only the Steelers look like a potential trouble spot for passing games. I hesitate to say it can't get worse but it would be tough to imagine. Waddle has finished inside the top-24 weekly scoring receivers just twice, which is only one fewer game than every other Denver wide receiver combined (Pat Bryant twice, Courtland Sutton once). I'm hoping the offense has a clearer vision for how to maximize the player they traded major draft picks for, as well as a more confident player under center.

In his last two games coming back from the team's Week 8 bye, Tank Dell has run a route on 55% and 54.8% of the dropbacks. The team has used guys like Xavier Hutchinson ahead of him and had him in a rotation with Marvin Harrison Jr. and Kayshon Boutte. There's a good chance this usage holds for Dell as he continues to work his way back from a serious injury. I'm still comfortable betting on him as an upside WR3 because he looks great on tape and is being used on downfield breaking routes. You just have to know you're dealing with a volatile projection with that playing time.

The Colts traded their best 2026 receiver at the deadline in Josh Downs and sent away a steady veteran in Keenan Allen. Their highly paid X-receiver, Alec Pierce, is on IR and I'm not expecting him to be back this year. Laquon Treadwell will lead this group in routes run as the every-down X-receiver going forward in a passing game that's been among the worst in the NFL, thanks to quarterback play. We don't need to fret the Colts' room much at this stage but I'm at least curious if any young players can flash to earn a role in 2027. Deion Burks is an interesting rookie with some juice who has gotten reps all year as a downfield Z-receiver.

The Jaguars are one of the toughest teams to prepare for in the NFL right now. This offense can run so many concepts out of multiple personnel groupings and has a litany of viable role players. Meanwhile, Jakobi Meyers and Parker Washington are two of the league's most productive receivers on throws of 10-20 air yards. In competitive games and good matchups, these two should roll, especially Washington.

Josh Downs' arrival in Kansas City paid immediate dividends for the offense. He was targeted five times on eight routes. Those numbers are only going up. The question is how high he can go and who comes off the field more often. Xavier Worthy seems like the natural answer given his play through three seasons, but his speed also brings tactical value by lifting coverages. Rashee Rice and Downs should be more complementary as they go. Additionally, Cyrus Allen's emergence as a talented route-runner and deep-ball option makes this even more complicated. I think Downs emerges as an every-week fantasy starter but we have playing time questions to answer to understand the ceiling.

Tre Tucker has been the team's best receiver but will be a free agent. He's a good player but mostly a vertical-only receiver. Meanwhile, Jalen Nailor has looked overstretched as an every-down starter. Second-year receiver Jack Bech is a nice role player but hasn't emerged as a highly-targeted option and missed a chunk of time with an injury. The Raiders will use the rest of the season to simply assess if any of these guys will be in the rotation in 2027, or if they will need to start fresh.

I've been ready to see more of Tre' Harris and less of Quentin Johnston since the first month of the season. Johnston has never fully developed and, frankly, looks like he's regressed in 2026. Meanwhile, Harris continues to flash a nice ability to separate and play multiple positions. He's run a route on more than 90% of the dropbacks just twice this year and compiled 60 yards on three catches in Week 4 and 86 on five last week. I'd love to watch Ladd McConkey take off in the back half, but I just worry he's not physically up for the task. He averages 2.25 yards per route run this year, so he's been good, but he just can't stay on the field.

Caleb Douglas leads all rookies with 2.27 yards per route run and the tape has been rock-solid. He is clearly a hit for the Dolphins. Now, let's find out how good he is. Malik Willis' injury will likely complicate things from a production standpoint. Yet, Douglas registered a season-high 155 air yards and caught three passes for 71 yards last week with Kyle McCord under center. Douglas has hit at least 70 yards in three of his last four games.

When the Patriots return from their bye week, they will do so without Drake Maye under center. The season is slowly slipping away for this team as they are buried under a mountain of injuries. It's just not going to be their year. The rest of the season should be dedicated to figuring out which members of the wide receiver room should have big roles in 2027 and beyond. That includes A.J. Brown, who has been unable to stay healthy and doesn't look right when he's been available. I'm not sure he'll be on the roster next year, which leaves Romeo Doubs and a collection of WR3 and WR4 types. This room needs an upgrade.

Despite playing in very different roles, we haven't seen many games where AD Mitchell and Omar Cooper Jr. both go off at the same time. That's especially tough when Garrett Wilson is available. The Jets have a strong trio of receivers with these guys on the roster and my sense is that they'll all be on the team in 2027. The rest of the way, we need to look at their individual matchups and success to try and pinpoint the right weeks Mitchell and Cooper can get going. This will also help us assess what the pecking order looks like behind Wilson next season in what, hopefully, is an improved overall ecosystem.

Germie Bernard looked like he might be one of those second-half-of-the-season rookie breakouts about a month ago. Yet, he's failed to clear 30 yards in any of his last three games. While I think he's a good player, you wonder if he has missed his window to cement a starting job. The Steelers will likely get veteran Michael Pittman Jr. back soon, along with Roman Wilson. The Bernard and Wilson playing time splits will be quite interesting since Wilson gave this team a real man coverage answer early in the year.

The Titans have found an answer to boosting Cam Ward in recent weeks: Tennessee has used no huddle on 45% of their snaps in each game on either side of the bye week. No other team is even above 18%. Ward operates well with tempo and from spread concepts. No surprise, this has resulted in Ward's two best games of the season. That's without Carnell Tate in the mix, who has been excellent on tape this year and a real weapon down the field. Once he's healthy, Tate's ability to quickly separate on routes over the middle in this new-look attack should result in some big weeks.

I'll be frank, this might be the bleakest wide receiver room I can remember in recent memory after they lost Michael Wilson for the season and placed Kendrick Bourne on IR. The only guy who is moderately interesting is Day 3 rookie, Reggie Virgil. He's a slower, poor man's Robbie Anderson from his days with the Panthers. Someone has to run routes for this team; catches may be optional.

We know what Drake London can do but not enough people are giving Jahan Dotson praise for the rebound season he is having. Dotson leads all wide receivers with 30-plus targets with 21.9 yards per catch. He's been a real nice answer for the team as a deep option down the field. His ability here has allowed the Falcons to keep London in a movement Z and slot-heavy role.

The Panthers need to change up their approach on offense. What worked to smash teams early in the season has become unworkable as pass-protection issues have cropped up. Bryce Young leads the NFL in pass attempts with pressure arriving in 2.5 seconds or faster. On those throws, Tetairoa McMillan averages almost 17 air yards per target and Jalen Coker is at almost 10. These receivers run long, developing routes to hit chunk plays and that's a thin line to walk when you can't hold up in protection.

This season, Rome Odunze averages 14.9 air yards per target, which leads the team by a wide margin and Luther Burden is at 7.4. The Bears aren't doing anything wrong here and both are thriving in their respective roles; there's just no overlap between the two. I believe Odunze can win in the short area as a chain-mover more often and Burden can get open in the intermediate area. Blending those would allow Odunze to be targeted more often and keep Burden involved for all four quarters.

At this rate, I'd be shocked if George Pickens returns to Dallas after an up-and-down season with the team on the franchise tag, especially with CeeDee Lamb turning in a dominant performance. Pickens will likely be motivated to put up more big performances after his Week 10 outing, which was the second-highest scoring game for a receiver all season. Keep an eye on his likely replacement, Ryan Flournoy, who has scored as a top-24 receiver in five of his last six games. He's already a viable option.

Over the last five weeks, Amon-Ra St. Brown and Jameson Williams account for 51% of the team's targets. Sam LaPorta is a relatively distant third at 14%. For much of the last two years, it's really been a push and pull between LaPorta and Williams for the second target option — rarely have they popped off together. The Lions have jumped their use of heavy personnel considerably in this post-bye span, which has contributed to the wideouts getting more burn, especially Williams, who runs a route on a team-high 90% of the dropbacks since Week 6. This is how we get more consistent weekly production from the volatile wideout.

Matthew Golden has been one of the breakout stars at the wide receiver position this year. He's the WR8 in points per game behind his teammate Christian Watson. That's been despite leading the league in drops by a considerable margin. Fantasy Points Data has charged Golden with 13 drops this year, almost double the amount of the second-highest receiver in AD Mitchell with eight. He's also won just three of his 11 contested targets. I'm always the first guy to suggest you just live with drop issues and continue to build the wideout's confidence so they can push past them. The Packers and Jordan Love appear to be taking that approach. If he can ever clean these issues up, the ceiling is quite high for Golden and he's already been excellent; scary proposition.

The Rams used a ton of 20 personnel in Week 10, with both Blake Corum and Kyren Williams on the field along with three wide receivers. For the most part, that was a collection of small speed-based options to hit big plays while the backs operate as checkdown outlets. It was a good bit of problem-solving by Sean McVay as injuries hit the receiver room but it's likely not sustainable. Now, if we can add Davante Adams to the mix as an intermediate answer to balance the scales, it could become a feature personnel grouping. The veteran could return in Week 12 or 13.

I'm not asking for much here. Justin Jefferson should continue to be the featured player. He registered 13 targets in Week 10. He had not seen 13-plus looks in a game since the Sam Darnold 2024 season. We just need complementary pieces to add pop to the other touches and layer in new dimensions to the offense with Kirk Cousins under center. I'll admit to enjoying the work of Myles Price and Tai Felton in place of some of the other guys who were previously putting the Vikings into schematic binds. Both averaged 6.0 yards or more after the catch per reception. Cousins having someone to spit the ball out to in the short area and get additional yards helps keep the offense on schedule.

Since Jordyn Tyson has been activated, he's taken over half his snaps from the slot and lined up off the ball on 80% of them. No wide receiver has been used in motion more than Tyson the last two weeks. He's been able to gobble up easy receptions while being targeted on 38% of his routes. That's awesome for his usage in fantasy but it was not a great plan for the Saints' offense, particularly in Week 10, when they ranked 23rd out of 28 teams in my offensive efficiency score. Tyson can get open in the intermediate area and Chris Olave shouldn't be left to run vertical stretch routes only. Tyson is an awesome player in this slot movement role but we can't overindex on the short passes, especially since he's not a run-after-catch threat.

Malik Nabers was looking close to 100% healthy prior to the Giants' Week 8 bye and enjoyed some strong moments with J.J. McCarthy against the Texans. The two games since have been less enjoyable. While it's easy to blame McCarthy for the issues and he's far from blameless, Nabers has left some plays on the field as well. I hold Nabers in extremely high regard and believe he is the next guy who could graduate to Tier 1 (Ja'Marr Chase, Jaxon Smith-Njigba, etc. grouping) at the receiver position. I'd love to see him knock down that door in the back-half of the season. He needs to rebound from two of his less inspiring outings in back-to-back weeks.

In Weeks 7 to 9, Makai Lemon owned a near 27% share of the Eagles targets. Despite four out-of-character drops, Lemon still averaged 61.7 yards per game and snagged 19 receptions. He was starting to look like the layup slot funnel you'd hope for from his archetype of receiver. However, the target competition is about to ramp up with DeVonta Smith and Dontayvion Wicks due back from injuries soon. How the Lemon and Smith, in particular, target distribution goes will inform us on how the situation may play out next season, as the Eagles play out the string for the rest of 2026.

The 49ers have the oldest wide receiver room I can remember with five guys over 31 years old taking snaps for the team in 2026. Yet, they keep rolling. Deebo Samuel Sr. has settled into a pure gadget role and barely operates as a true receiver. Keenan Allen arrived in the middle of last week and immediately earned a first down on 19.1% of his routes. He's going to be quite helpful down the stretch. When Mike Evans is healthy, he's still a fantastic starting X-receiver.

Jaxon Smith-Njigba has 1,207 yards on 79 catches (109 targets) through 10 weeks of the NFL season. You'd do backflips if your favorite sleeper receiver did that over a full season. Smith-Njigba is on pace to clear 2,000 yards this season. He'd be the first wideout in NFL history to do it. Based on his own individual play despite teams crafting coverages to stop him, and the lack of a true secondary threat, there's no reason to expect him to slow down anytime soon.

I'm very confident that Emeka Egbuka is a good NFL player but the Buccaneers need him to be great. We've yet to see that version of the player this season. To be fair, Egbuka has been dogged by injuries since training camp and the guy we saw against the Bears prior to the bye week was the worst version I've watched on tape in 2026. Hopefully a week off helps him get back to form as he heads into a good matchup against the Lions defense. Egbuka's 20.8% slot rate in Week 9 was the lowest of the season; that needs to rise in future weeks.

Since the team returned from the bye, Antonio Williams has led the team in routes run. He looks good on tape and gets easy separation in the intermediate area. However, he hasn't been consistently involved in the offense (16% target share). The Commanders spread the ball around, and he's competing with an excellent receiver in Stefon Diggs, who leads the team over the last three weeks with a 25% target-per-route rate. Williams can still make some big plays but we need him to get the ball more consistently to be an every-week option in fantasy. He does remain and excellent buy in dynasty leagues.

#2852. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#2851. Inside India newsletter: Foreign flows helped the market recover. Now it faces the inflation test

Hello, this is Priyanka Salve, writing to you from Singapore.

Welcome to the latest edition of "Inside India" — your one-stop destination for stories and developments from the world's fastest-growing large economy.

As global portfolio managers look to diversify into non-artificial intelligence trades, India, with its strong economic growth and improved corporate earnings, is emerging as a top pick. But the country's inflation, which has been rising for 12 straight months, could impede inflows.

Any thoughts on today's newsletter? Share them with the team.

Foreign investors are returning to Indian equities in record numbers, but the country's accelerating inflation, which last month breached the central bank's comfort level for the first time in two years, could spoil the party.

India's retail inflation rose to 6.09% ​in October, crossing the Reserve Bank of India's 6% upper limit, driven by surging food and transport costs.

Experts told CNBC that businesses have already started passing on increased costs to consumers, with signs of slowing demand visible in the rural economy and expected to hurt urban consumption by early next year.

"Rising inflation will squeeze household real incomes, while higher interest rates will weigh on investment and consumption, potentially putting pressure on corporate earnings," Alexandra Hermann Prasad, lead economist at Oxford Economics, told CNBC.

That could "temper foreign investor enthusiasm in the near term, even if India's structural growth prospects remain attractive," she added.

High-frequency indicators like car sales and credit card transactions softened in September while air passenger traffic declined for the fourth month in a row, Indian brokerage HDFC Securities said in a report on Tuesday.

As per data from the Federation of Automobile Dealers Associations, released on Nov. 6, car sales fell 14.2% year on year in October. The industry body said that its "pressing concern is inventory" with dealers, which was elevated at 45 days in September and October, as against the usual range of 30 days. The inflation print "is not good," Gaura Sengupta, chief economist at India's IDFC First Bank, told CNBC, adding that inflation pressures led by supply-side shocks are spreading, and if that persists, it will affect corporate margins and household consumption.

She expects the RBI to raise benchmark rates by 25 basis points each over the next three monetary policies, taking the terminal interest rate to 6.25% by April next year.

The tightening of monetary policy is also likely to slow the recovery of the private investment cycle, denting India's economic outlook, experts said.

So far in November, foreign investors have pumped in more than $10 billion net in Indian equities. If that sustains, it will be the highest ever foreign capital inflow in a single month.

This would be a sharp contrast to earlier this year in March when foreign investors pulled a record $12 billion from India on worries that the energy shock from the Iran war could squeeze the economy and slow growth.

India's growth story, however, has remained intact so far, with corporate earnings showing a sharp improvement over the last two quarters. That resilience, alongside a rotation from AI names, has drawn investors back to India, as they look to diversify into non-artificial intelligence trades.

The fall in oil prices and the easing of tensions between Iran and the U.S. after the midterm poll results have also worked in India's favor, though experts warn that it is still too early to call November's rebound of foreign capital "a structural reversal."

"November's return of capital inflows primarily reflects lower oil prices, changing global risk sentiment following the US midterm elections, and growing concerns about the sustainability of the AI cycle, rather than a clear structural shift towards Indian assets," Hanna Luchnikava-Schorsch, head of Asia-Pacific Economics at S&P Global Market Intelligence, told CNBC.

That makes rising inflation a big worry for sustained inflows. Higher inflation could further weaken household demand, prompt monetary tightening, and slow earnings growth, making the return on Indian assets "less attractive," Luchnikava-Schorsch said.

India relies on imports to meet 85% of its crude needs and around 50% of its supplies transited through the Strait of Hormuz before the Iran war. Energy supply disruptions due to the Iran war have led to a sharp rise in inflation across several major economies in the world, including India.

"What matters more for attracting stable, long-term capital, and for weathering short-term swings in flows, is whether India can sustain the high growth path it is on," Madhavi Bokil, executive director, credit strategy & standards at Moody's Ratings, told CNBC, adding that higher inflation is a risk to that path.

India's inflation crosses 6% in October after rising for 12 straight monthsIndia's retail inflation rose to 6.09% ​in October, crossing the Reserve Bank of India's 6% limit for the first time under the new series, owing to surging food prices. Inflation has been on the rise for 12 straight months in the South Asian country.

India raises export levies on diesel, aviation turbine fuel amid high inflationIndia reportedly raised export levies on diesel and jet fuel on Monday, while keeping the ‌duty on petrol exports unchanged. The increased levies on exports of key fuel items ​come on the back of rising inflation in India.

Nov.23: HSBC composite flash PMI for November.

#2850. Lakers star gets honest on public scrutiny weighing NBA players down

Being an NBA player comes with its many perks, but it also comes with some struggles.

Lakers star Walker Kessler opened up about witnessing how players lose their love for basketball amid criticism and other factors that come with being in the spotlight.

Walker Kessler #14 of the Los Angeles Lakers looks on during the game against the Utah Jazz on November 18, 2026 at Crypto.Com Arena in Los Angeles, California. NBAE via Getty Images

"There's definitely some out there that don't love it as much. Unfortunately, some players go into the league loving it and then all the scrutiny, all the extra stuff, they kind of get blinded or distracted by that.

"So they lose the love of the game because they focus so much on all the other stuff, and it's sad to see," Kessler said on The Old Man and the Three podcast.

"You can see it sometimes with a player — they just don't seem like they're having joy out there. It's not even like they're pouting, it's just like they feel like they've got a weight on their shoulder… There's definitely guys out there that don't love to play."

Luka Doncic (77), Walker Kessler (14), and Quentin Grimes (5) of the Los Angeles Lakers walk off the court during a game against the Toronto Raptors on Nov. 1, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

Winning, of course, surely helps the entire Lakers roster feel confident and capable in their individual roles with LA. However, the chemistry of the entire team can't be denied.

#2849. Amid recent banking outages, should you start carrying cash again? Top advisors weigh in

For years, Americans have moved away from using physical U.S. currency in favor of credit, debit and digital payments. But amid the recent outages affecting some consumer banking services, experts say it's wise to revisit cash.

JPMorgan Chase on Monday reported a technical issue that disrupted some consumer banking services, with ripple effects extending to several major U.S. banks and the peer-to-peer payments network Zelle. A day later, a separate outage affected Apple, temporarily disrupting Apple Pay and iCloud, the service that keeps data available across various Apple devices.

Those reports came on the heels of separate, widespread issues with Microsoft Outlook and communication systems used by air traffic control centers.

As technology outages expose vulnerabilities across parts of the U.S. economy, some top advisors say consumers should reconsider keeping physical money on hand.

"This is about preparedness, not panic," said David La Placa, CEO of Intellectus Partners in San Francisco, which ranked No. 92 on CNBC's Financial Advisor 100 list for 2026. "I think keeping some cash at home is a reasonable starting point for many households."

La Placa advises consumers to "build redundancy" into their financial lives.

"Maintain access to at least a second financial institution, keep a backup credit card from a different issuer and make sure you have more than one way to access liquidity," he said.

A client's cash management should be diversified, said Seth Fierston, a certified financial planner and co-president of Fierston Financial Group in West Hartford, Connecticut. The firm ranked No. 22 on CNBC's Financial Advisor 100 list.

Having multiple accounts at brokerages or banks and various ways to move money, including Automated Clearing House, or ACH, wire, check, Venmo or Zelle, "can help mitigate technology risk," Fierston said. "One of our guiding principles is flexibility."

When it comes to everyday transactions, Americans are increasingly relying on digital forms of payment.

These days, only about 46% of Americans carry cash with them always or most of the time, according to a survey of nearly 10,000 adults conducted between May and June by the Pew Research Center, a nonpartisan research group.

Roughly 42% of U.S. adults said they don't use cash for any purchases in a typical week, up from 24% in 2015, Pew found.

Young adults are the least likely to carry physical currency. Among adults under 30, 51% said they go completely cashless, according to the survey.

"I recommend to my clients that they always keep three days of average cash spend on hand given the uncertainty of life in general," said Tim Lootens, CFP, managing director at Chilton Capital Management in Houston, which ranked No. 7 on this year's Financial Advisor 100.

Considering the recent technical troubles, Lootens said consumers should keep even more cash at the ready.

"We've had the Microsoft Outlook outage, the FAA ground shutdown and now banking disruption," he said. "This makes me think five to seven days may be a better number."

The right amount is as psychological as it is financial, he added. "The ability to access cash and having comfort knowing it is there when needed is so important," he said.

Brian Vendig, president and chief investment officer at MJP Wealth Advisors, which ranked No. 42 on CNBC's Financial Advisor 100, also said he recommends holding a dollar amount that corresponds with a household's discretionary living expenses. He advised having enough for at least one to two weeks, depending on their needs.

While there is little precedent for a banking or payment system outage lasting more than a few business days, Vendig said, a one- to two-week cash reserve provides a "reasonable buffer" for essentials such as groceries, gas and other necessities, if electronic payments become unavailable.

"We apply this range because it is also an emotional concern for most people, and they might want to have a little extra saved at home due to the size of their household and having a little extra 'peace of mind' guarantee," he said.

Vendig said he recommends stashing extra cash in a secure location at home for emergency needs.

Vendig also said consumers should be vigilant in ensuring their electronic devices have the most recent software security patches installed.

Criminals will try to exploit moments of uncertainty, experts say.

"Consumers should assume they will be targeted," said Craig Timm, senior director of anti-money laundering at ACAMS, an anti-financial crime group.

"[This week's] reported bank outages are likely to cause widespread confusion and fear among consumers about losing access to their money," he said. "Criminals will try to exploit that anxiety by pushing victims to take urgent action to protect their funds or restore account access."

As always, "stay alert, never click on links in unexpected bank messages and never share personal information such as passwords or PINs," he advised. "If you need to reach your bank, use a trusted, official channel," Timm said.

#2848. Mark Stone sets Vegas’ all-time points record during Golden Knights-Stars game

Mark Stone sets Vegas' all-time points record during Golden Knights-Stars game appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

There is a new points king for the Vegas Golden Knights franchise. On Thursday, Mark Stone finally assumes the solo spot atop the Knights' all-time list for most points in team history.

Stone entered the game tied with former Golden Knights star Jonathan Marchessault, who now plays for the Nashville Predators, with 417 points in a Vegas uniform.

The 34-year-old Stone broke the tie in the second period of Tuesday's game against the Dallas Stars at T-Mobile Arena in Las Vegas with an assist to a power-play goal by Victor Olofsson — Vegas' first goal of the evening.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

Here is a look at that monumental assist from Stone.

Stone tied Marchessault for the record with a goal in the Golden Knights' 6-3 home loss to the Vancouver Canucks last Sunday. He went scoreless in the following Vegas game, which was a 4-3 overtime loss to the Stars in Las Vegas on Tuesday.

Stone played for six-plus seasons with the Ottawa Senators to start his NHL career, but he's clearly found a home with the Golden Knights. Vegas acquired Stone from a trade with the Senators along with Tobias Lindberg. In return, the Sens got Erik Brännström, Oscar Lindberg and a 2020 second-round pick. Not long after the trade, the Golden Knights secured Stone for the long term, signing him to an eight-year $76 million contract extension.

Since then, Stone has become one of the top players ever for the relatively young Vegas franchise, which he also helped the Golden Knights reach the Stanley Cup Final three times, including when the team won it all in 2023.

#2846. Anthropic IPO: Investors who flip the stock may face brokerage penalty

Retail investors with access to Anthropic's initial public offering who sell the stock quickly after buying could get penalized by their brokerage firm.

Anthropic said in an IPO prospectus that it expected to make shares available to retail investors through various brokerages, including Charles Schwab, Fidelity, Robinhood, SoFi, E-Trade and some of their respective affiliates.

Follow CNBC's live coverage of Anthropic's IPO and Nasdaq debut

These broker-dealers have "anti-flipping" policies meant to discourage investors who purchase IPO shares — including those of Anthropic — from dumping them quickly. Charles Schwab is the only one that doesn't have such a policy in place.

"One purpose is to discourage 'flippers' who are just buying with the hope of making a quick profit," Jay Ritter, director of The IPO Initiative at the University of Florida, said in an email. "Their selling could put downward pressure on the price once trading starts."

Investors who run afoul of anti-flipping rules may be prohibited from accessing future IPOs, perhaps for several months or even more than a year for repeat offenders. In the worst case, the brokerage may bar them from all future IPOs.

These policies apply only to retail investors who place an order with their brokerage firm to buy the Anthropic IPO at its offering price — something many investors don't have access to.

For example, Fidelity customers are eligible to participate in the Anthropic IPO if they have at least $25,000 in certain assets, the firm said. Even then, customers are "evaluated and ranked" based on factors such as account assets to determine who can access limited shares that may be available, it said.

Importantly, anti-flipping policies don't apply to retail investors who access shares later, once Anthropic stock starts trading on a public exchange, Ritter said.

Of the retail brokerage firms listed in Anthropic's prospectus, Charles Schwab appears to be the only one without an explicit anti-flipping policy.

"At this time, Schwab does not apply a flipping restriction to any IPOs unless required by an issuer," Margaret Farrell, a company spokesperson, wrote in an email. "This is subject to change at Schwab's discretion."

She confirmed that an anti-flipping policy doesn't apply to the Anthropic IPO.

Here's how they could penalize IPO flippers.

E-Trade said it "prefers investors hold shares for at least 30 calendar days."

However, its policy doesn't lay out an explicit penalty for failing to do so.

Selling or transferring out of an IPO within 30 days "may affect eligibility to participate in other new issues in the future," according to its policy. "E*TRADE reserves the right to exclude customers from future participation."

Investors who are allocated Anthropic shares and sell within the first 15 calendar days from the start of trading in the secondary market won't be able to participate in future stock IPOs through Fidelity for a defined period of time, it said:

"The first day clients can sell without being labeled a 'flipper' is the 16th calendar day after the IPO trades," Fidelity said.

Robinhood's policy stipulates that selling IPO shares within 30 days of the IPO is considered flipping.

Such investors "may be prevented from participating in IPO access for 60 days," it said.

SoFi said that selling an IPO position within the first 30 calendar days after trading starts "may affect your ability to participate in future IPOs on SoFi."

#2844. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2843. Houston Texans vs. Indianapolis Colts ‘TNF’ Week 11 live game chat

Look what we have here, Rumblers: It's a primetime matchup between a pair of AFC teams. The trouble here is that this is a terrible matchup, featuring teams that are decidedly anything other than apex hunters.

With that, here's your open thread for Week 11 of Thursday Night Football. Maybe this one also turns into a decent game, like a few suspect snooze-fests in primetime before it. As always, be kind to one another — and in all things… Go Bills!

#2841. Palo Alto stock jumps on earnings beat and company's big bet on securing open-weight models

Palo Alto Networks shares rose 6% on Thursday as businesses boosted spending on cybersecurity products and investors cheered the company's acquisition of a startup that pushes it into securing open-weight artificial intelligence models.

The company beat Wall Street's estimates for the fiscal first quarter. Revenue jumped 39% from a year ago and Palo Alto lifted its guidance for the year.

Recent outages at Microsoft, banks and the Federal Aviation Administration have left businesses reeling. While U.S. officials haven't detected any malicious activity, the disruptions raise concerns over aging equipment and infrastructure. Many businesses are scrambling to patch vulnerabilities at a faster clip than ever before to keep pace with the growing risks of AI-powered cyberattacks.

Those fears have led more businesses to consolidate platforms, which is fueling growth in areas like firewall and AI security, Palo Alto Networks CEO Nikesh Arora told CNBC's "Mad Money" on Wednesday.

"They're going to have to make sure they get the best players in the market to come support them because this is no longer child's play," Arora said.

Alongside earnings, Palo Alto announced a $4.5 billion deal to buy Applied Compute, a startup that helps train customized open-weight models. It's one of several recent deals in Arora's aggressive acquisition spree and marks a significant shift beyond cybersecurity.

"Rather than being one player out of 10 in the industry serving compute, we want to be the one player with Palo Alto that delivers secure compute and security capability," Arora told CNBC.

Palo Alto expects the deal to add more than $1 billion in annual recurring revenue to its next-generation security business by the 2029 fiscal year.

Analysts at Bank of America touted the deal as a "bold move" to expand beyond securing AI into training open-weight models, allowing Palo Alto to lead the next phase of the AI revolution.

This "appears consistent with PANW's 'long-game' - become indispensable beyond cyber into multidisciplinary IT domains across AI-value-chain," wrote analysts at Citigroup.

#2840. LeBron Log No. 5: Popcorn in hand, a viral chorus and ‘basketball AI'

LeBron Log No. 5: Popcorn in hand, a viral chorus and 'basketball AI' originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

LeBron James spent Monday night in street clothes.

He still managed to argue a call with popcorn in his hand, practice his golf swing during a timeout and reject a report about himself before the Sixers left Brooklyn with a win.

That followed a Sunday afternoon in which James passed Jason Kidd on the NBA's career assists list and received an unusual description from Pacers head coach Rick Carlisle.

Welcome to the fifth edition of the LeBron Log.

James sat out the Sixers' 135-123 win over the Nets because of low back and sciatica management.

It was his fourth absence through 15 games. James has yet to play both ends of a back-to-back this season after logging nearly 33 minutes Sunday against Indiana.

He watched Monday's game from the end of the Sixers' bench in a yellow hat and an "Ohio Baby" sweatshirt. James snacked from a small bag before switching to popcorn.

When Joel Embiid believed he was fouled on a first-half drive, James stood and pleaded his case to official Jacyn Goble without putting down the box. He also joined conversations between teammates and officials during several timeouts.

James found another way to pass a third-quarter stoppage. As "Hot To Go" played inside Barclays Center, he practiced his golf swing near the bench.

His most pointed contribution came on Instagram.

An ESPN story by Dave McMenamin cited a Sixers source who said James had asked the coaching staff to remain patient while he worked toward finding his rhythm and conditioning by December.

James reposted ESPN's graphic and rejected the account.

"🧢! Those words never came out of my mouth!" he wrote.

The Sixers improved to 4-0 without James. They have beaten the Bucks, Hornets, Raptors and Nets in the games he has missed.

Brooklyn pulled within five points during the final five minutes Monday, putting the Sixers in another clutch game.

Philadelphia has reached 10 clutch wins more quickly than almost any team since the statistic began being tracked in 1996-97. The 2026-27 Sixers are the second team in that span with at least 10 such victories through their first 15 games.

The other was the 1997-98 Atlanta Hawks, who had 12.

James was also on the last team before Philadelphia to open a season with at least 10 consecutive clutch wins. The Lakers started 13-0 in those games last season.

James did not play in the Sixers' latest close finish, but he has now been part of the last two teams to start at least 10-0 under those circumstances.

Before Sunday's game, Carlisle explained why he tries to limit his play calls against James.

"LeBron James is one of the reasons we try not to have play calls, 'cause he knows all of them," Carlisle said. "He's a walking NBA basketball AI machine. That's what he is."

James followed with 15 points, 11 assists and seven rebounds in the Sixers' 131-124 win.

His pass to VJ Edgecombe for a layup with 1:19 remaining gave him 12,092 career assists and moved him past Kidd for third in NBA history. Only Chris Paul and John Stockton remain ahead of him.

Noah Levick wrote more about James' milestone and the anticipation behind his passes.

When told what Carlisle had called him, James appreciated the description.

"So, yeah, Rick is great," James said. "He's one of the greatest coaches of all time, and I've had my battles with him over the years. That's super humbling coming from a guy like that."

Philadelphia has found its soundtrack for James' biggest home moments.

The Sixers have played the viral "LeBron, LeBron, LeBron" song after several of his baskets, including his late putback against the Knicks on Oct. 30.

The refrain comes from a version of Steve Lacy's "Oh Yeah?" that replaces "Come on" with James' name. The Baylor University Men's Choir performed its own rendition, helping the song spread across social media.

Lacy later joined in. During an Oct. 15 concert at Freedom Mortgage Pavilion in Camden, he performed the LeBron version as the crowd sang along.

James has noticed Philadelphia's use of it.

"I've seen it. I've heard it. It's pretty hilarious, to be honest," James said. "I heard it towards the end of a game when we were winning. I made a play, and I heard that song. I was just chuckling to myself on the inside."

#2839. From recovery to race day in Philly: Former addict credits running with saving his life

A local man, who credits running with saving his life, will participate in the Philadelphia Marathon for the fourth time on Sunday morning.

Between this weekend's full marathon, half marathon, and 8k, a record-breaking 40,000 runners will toe the starting line along the Ben Franklin Parkway.

Kellen Matthews will be one of the 20,000 athletes completing the full marathon.

The 35-year-old told Action News he has always enjoyed playing sports, but he didn't find a love for the sport of running until he found himself at a crossroads.

When he was 14 years old, Matthews was introduced to prescription painkillers.

"By the time I was 25, I was an IV drug user and just feeling all the shame, guilt, hopeless, all the things that come with substance use disorder," he explained.

With the assistance of his girlfriend, Cass, who is now his wife, Matthews went to a treatment facility in Texas. He said that's where a doctor introduced him to the sport of running.

"The first year of my recovery, I ran a mile every day," he said.

Over the past 10 years, those miles have added up for Matthews, who has run almost every race you can imagine.

"I feel like running gives me the exact opposite feeling that being affected by substance abuse disorder gives me... it gives you pride and something to build on," he told Action News' Caroline Goggin.

Matthews has built a career surrounding this sport. He has used his story to become a content creator and to start a run club called the "Recovery Run Club."

"It's kind of for anyone in some sort of recovery - could be eating disorders, substance use, domestic violence. Whatever it is that you're struggling with and looking to recover from, you can come out, find community, and get some exercise," he explained.

Now, he is the face of a statewide public awareness campaign designed to teach Pennsylvanians that recovery is possible.

"I really can't put into words how proud I am of myself because I couldn't have imagined in a million years that I would be in the position that I'm in now," he said.

This weekend, with his wife and daughter there to cheer him on, Matthews will take on the Philadelphia Marathon - fueled by a complicated past that has led him to this moment.

"I often use my experience as fuel when things get tough," he said. "Helps keep things in perspective that I'm choosing to do hard things."

#2838. Hard-luck Rams catch break in Puka Nacua suspension

It figures that in a season this sideways, the best break the Rams have caught in weeks came from the people who just suspended their best player.

Somewhere in the NFL offices, somebody signed off on a three-game suspension for Puka Nacua. Somewhere in Los Angeles, somebody had to look at the calendar and think, well, if you insist.

Los Angeles Rams wide receiver Puka Nacua reacts after winning an NFL football game against the Washington Commanders in Landover, Md., Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. AP Photo/Stephanie Scarbrough

Nacua can serve all three games while he's on injured reserve. The shoulder pads were going to stay in his locker anyway. When he's healthy enough to put them back on, his suspension will already be behind him.

How often this season have the Rams been able to say something worked out about as well as they could have hoped?

Certainly nobody was saying it after the Denver game. The Rams had a game they should have won, the kind you circle when you're trying to figure out how a team with this much talent ended up having to fight this hard just to keep its head above water.

Just look at who has been hit by injuries. Myles Garrett. Nacua. Davante Adams. Cornerbacks Jaylen Watson and Trent McDuffie. That's a whole lot of the blueprint spending time on the injury report. The Rams built this team to pressure quarterbacks, cover receivers and make defenses account for two dangerous targets.

Injuries have reached into every one of those plans. Sean McVay has spent too much of this season figuring out what's left to work with each week.

They've also had more experience than anybody around here wanted finding out what the offense looks like with one of its best players standing next to McVay. There's a reason a coach spends all week drawing up ways to get the ball to somebody. The guy behind him might know exactly where to line up. There's still the matter of what happens once the ball gets there.

With Nacua, sometimes the best part of the play starts right about where it should end. A defender gets his arms around him a yard short of the sticks, and somehow it's first down. Try finding that on the call sheet.

That still leaves the Packers, the Chiefs and the 49ers on the schedule without Nacua. Go ahead and read those names back to McVay and see how lucky he feels.

Head Coach Sean McVay of the Los Angeles Rams looks on during the fourth quarter against the Washington Commanders in Landover, Maryland, Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. Getty Images

Except the Rams were going to play those games without him anyway. And that's where the calendar did them a favor. They've already been figuring out how to win without Nacua. Now they just have to do it a little longer.

Maybe the catches come from three different guys against Green Bay. Maybe the running backs have to carry them against Kansas City. By the time San Francisco rolls around, nobody will care if the same formula worked twice.

Now imagine getting word that Nacua is healthy again and having to explain to the rest of the locker room that it'll be another three Sundays before anybody gets to see him play. That was the danger. The timing of the suspension took it off the table.

At some point on that flight, everybody involved had to let go of whatever they thought the mission was going to look like. They had duct tape. They had some awfully smart people on the ground. They had a bunch of stuff on board originally intended for other purposes. What could they piece together that would get three astronauts back to Earth?

Get there however you can. Win an ugly one. Ask a backup to give you one good afternoon. Give the defense a lead late and let somebody on that side close out the kind of game that got away against Denver. Then come back Monday and see who can practice.

For all that's gone sideways, the Rams are still alive. And the team an opponent draws in January could look a whole lot more like the one we expected in September than the one McVay is trying to hold together in November.

Puka Nacua #12 of the Los Angeles Rams celebrates a fourth-quarter touchdown with Head Coach Sean McVay in Landover, Maryland, Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. Getty Images

Nobody asks how much duct tape it took once you land. And if the Rams get there with Nacua healthy, good luck to the defensive coordinator who spent the last month watching film of an offense that had to make do without him.

All the Rams need is a way in. From there, they have a chance to run the table. Funny to think, after everything that's happened to them, who just helped keep the door open.

#2837. Amazon app warns New York of higher costs if Mamdani delivery law passes

Amazon is taking its fight against a New York City delivery bill, which has been backed by Mayor Zohran Mamdani, to its shopping website and app.

Some users who open the company's shopping app or website report being served with pop-ups and banners warning that the New York City Council "could add up to $5 per order" if it passes Intro 518, or the Delivery Protection Act.

The bill would require Amazon to employ its army of third-party delivery companies directly, disrupting the contractor model it has relied on since 2018. Amazon uses a network of thousands of small companies, which often work solely for Amazon, to handle the last-mile portion of shipments, in an effort to speed up deliveries and control more of its logistics operations.

"If this legislation passes, we'd no longer be able to work with them, and we'd be forced to consider moving our operations outside of the city," Amazon's in-app message states. "That would likely mean slower and more expensive delivery for millions of customers in New York City, while also threatening small businesses and their thousands of employees."

The proposed legislation was introduced in February and is awaiting review by City Council Speaker Julie Menin.

Amazon also posted a statement on its company website where it often publishes its policy positions. But rarely has the company used its valuable e-commerce site and app real estate to push its perspectives. Other tech companies including Uber and Lyft have similarly used their apps to advocate for or against legislation in the past.

Amazon's pop-up notices and a stationary banner on its store homepage direct users to a form where they can "contact your council member to oppose Intro 518."

According to Amazon, 20,000 customers sent emails to city council members in the first six hours that the pop-up appeared.

Menin told CNBC in a statement that Amazon's claim it will need to raise prices due to the proposed legislation "defies logic."

"We are crafting legislation that regulates Amazon and protects workers, without impacting prices or jobs," Menin said. "If Amazon thinks this deceptive campaign will effectively threaten New Yorkers and intimidate this City Council, they are dead wrong."

Amazon spokesperson Kelly Nantel said in a statement that the company put up the notices to be "transparent with our customers" about what the bill "would cause them if it passes as written."

"If customers are concerned, one of the most impactful things they can do is contact their City Council member and ask them to oppose this bill," Nantel said.

The Delivery Protection Act has received a swell of support from labor groups, including the International Brotherhood of Teamsters, which has sought to unionize Amazon delivery and warehouse workers.

Mamdani endorsed the bill in August, saying Amazon's subcontractor model "leaves workers vulnerable and corporations free to avoid accountability for reckless conditions on city streets." He's argued the bill would lead to safer working conditions for drivers, and allow the city to "regulate last-mile operations."

Amazon disputed Mamdani's claims, saying the bill would put Delivery Service Partner jobs "at risk without improving safety." It also argued that other businesses and governments use contractors in their operations, and said the DSP model has allowed it to meet growing customer demand.

"It works because local entrepreneurs — people rooted in their communities — deliver a better experience," Amazon said. "They invest in their teams, their routes, and their neighborhoods. That's not a liability play."

Amazon has lobbied heavily against the bill, while logistics and business groups have launched local advertising campaigns, and warned they may sue the city if the bill passes.

#2836. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2835. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

Catellier ranks No. 667 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.60%. See Williams Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2834. Spurs’ Victor Wembanyama explains hilarious jump-shot dunk during Clippers game

Spurs' Victor Wembanyama explains hilarious jump-shot dunk during Clippers game appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The Los Angeles Clippers gave the San Antonio Spurs fits during their Thursday night contest, but in the end, the Spurs locked in and got enough breathing room en route to a 124-112 victory. With less than four minutes remaining in the game and the Spurs already up by double digits, Victor Wembanyama got out in transition after a missed jumpshot Rui Hachimura — setting himself up for one of the most incredibly hilarious plays in NBA history.

Wembanyama, who stands at 7-foot-4, can dunk the ball whenever he's within a few feet of the basket. But with no Clippers defender in sight, the Spurs star tried something new. Instead of slamming the ball (throwing it down while touching the rim), Wembanyama made use of his freakish size to shoot a jumpshot from point-blank range in a way that's similar to how the layman can dunk the ball in puny basketball arcade machines.

But Wembanyama simply wasn't showboating. He did that insane play for a reason. After the game, the Spurs star revealed that he's protecting himself from making the blisters on his fingers worse. Thus, whenever possible, he looks to avoid throwing it down.

"I'm not gonna lie, I've had this blister forever and it's killing me. And every time I dunk it, kills me. If I can avoid it, I'll avoid it. It's actually so painful," Wembanyama said in his postgame presser, via Oh No He Didn't on X.

Wembanyama entered the night with 69 dunks already on the season in just 15 games, giving him an average of nearly five dunks per contest. The force and the regularity with which he throws it down must be leading to those blisters.

Nonetheless, his combination of size, length, timing, and soft hands make him the perfect lob target for the Spurs. They generate so many easy buckets due to Wembanyama's skillset and physical stature, and it will be in the Spurs' best interest to try and help their star man heal from those painful blisters as quickly as possible.

#2832. Trump bought Nvidia, Palantir, SpaceX in September as federal deals, White House ties grew

President Donald Trump's investment accounts bought shares of Nvidia, Palantir and Elon Musk's SpaceX in September, around the time the companies secured major federal contracts and their executives met with the president, according to a newly released financial disclosure.

The 25-page filing shows 751 purchases and sales totaling between roughly $40.9 million and $148.1 million, according to a CNBC analysis. Purchases accounted for at least $29.2 million and sales at least $11.7 million. The filing reports transactions in broad dollar ranges, leaving the exact amounts and value of Trump's holdings unclear.

Among the transactions were Palantir purchases before the company received two federal contracts worth $42.5 million combined, according to contracting records, SpaceX investments shortly before and after a $946 million NASA contract expansion, and Nvidia trades as its then-CEO, Jensen Huang, cultivated a close relationship with Trump.

The trades underscore a striking feature of Trump's presidency: His personal fortune remains invested in companies whose fortunes his administration can shape. His accounts reported more than 21,000 trades in 2025, compared with roughly 500 during his entire first term. Recent presidents have generally divested individual stocks or used blind trusts to avoid potential conflicts.

The White House has told CNBC previously Trump and his family have no control over the trades, which outside financial managers handle independently. The Trump Organization says the arrangement is designed to avoid even the appearance of a conflict. There is no evidence Trump directed the transactions or that his managers knew government decisions in advance.

White House spokesman Davis Ingle said that Trump's portfolio is independently managed and that neither the president nor his family has any input on trades. "All investment decisions are made entirely by independent managers," Ingle told CNBC.

The Trump Organization did not immediately respond to a request for comment.

The timing was particularly notable at Palantir, the artificial intelligence software maker whose technology is used by U.S. military, intelligence and civilian agencies.

Trump's accounts bought between $15,001 and $50,000 in Palantir shares on Sept. 2, followed by another purchase of between $1,001 and $15,000 on Sept. 9.

A week later, the Department of Housing and Urban Development awarded Palantir a contract with $10.4 million in initial obligations for AI-powered grant oversight and fraud detection.

On Sept. 29, the Department of Homeland Security awarded Palantir another $32.1 million software contract, bringing the two awards to roughly $42.5 million. That same day, CEO Alex Karp joined Trump, Musk, Huang and other technology executives at the White House for a meeting on artificial intelligence.

Trump's accounts also bought shares and debt in SpaceX in the weeks surrounding a major NASA contract award.

On Sept. 1, the accounts purchased between $100,001 and $250,000 in SpaceX debt carrying a 5.35% interest rate. Eight days later, they bought between $1,001 and $15,000 in shares of the major NASA and Pentagon contractor.

On Sept. 18, NASA announced a $946 million expansion of its SpaceX contract, adding three astronaut missions to the International Space Station and bringing the agreement's total value to $5.92 billion. The agency had signaled plans for additional missions in May.

Six days after the announcement, Trump's accounts bought another $15,001 to $50,000 in SpaceX shares on the same day Musk attended Trump's White House state dinner for Chinese President Xi Jinping. The filing does not show whether the purchase came before or after the dinner.

The trades followed an even larger SpaceX investment in August, when Trump's accounts bought between $1 million and $5 million in the company's debt just two days before he signed a policy aimed at expanding commercial space transportation.

Trump's accounts also continued buying Nvidia shares as Huang drew closer to the president — a relationship now under congressional scrutiny over Huang's ties to a woman suspected of spying for China.

The September filing shows four Nvidia purchases totaling between $152,004 and $380,000, including two on Sept. 2 worth as much as $350,000 combined.

The accounts also sold between $1,001 and $15,000 in Nvidia shares on Sept. 24, the day Huang also attended the state dinner with Xi.

The trades bring Trump's Nvidia transactions to at least 108 since his return to office in 2025, worth between $27.2 million and $97.9 million, extending a CNBC analysis published Monday.

The relationship between the president and Nvidia's then-CEO has drawn fresh scrutiny after The New York Times reported that intelligence officials warned Trump about Huang's relationship with a suspected Chinese spy. Trump has denied being briefed. Huang resigned Nov. 12 following revelations about his personal relationships.

The September filing also shows substantial trading beyond the three companies. The largest reported transaction was a Sept. 23 purchase of between $5 million and $25 million in Vanguard Dividend Appreciation ETF shares.

That same day, Trump's accounts sold between $1 million and $5 million each in Comcast, McDonald's, Home Depot and Fidelity National Information Services, along with between $500,001 and $1 million each in Nike and Constellation Brands.

On the buy side, Trump's accounts purchased between $250,001 and $500,000 in TJX shares on each of two consecutive days, Sept. 15 and 16. They also bought between $117,004 and $330,000 in Tesla shares across four transactions, along with smaller investments in CoreWeave, Intel, Microsoft and Apple.
